ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e1965	Please, Minister, answer!
ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e1980	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I can say that a shift in the Austrian environmental programme ÖPUL 2000 is not planned. The draft OPUL 2000 has been completed in good time and is before the Commission for the negotiation of the notification. The approval of the OPUL 2000 will be based on the rural development Regulation. It must be assumed that this will take some time because 15 countries have submitted their programmes. Austria has therefore proposed to the Commission that those farmers whose ÖPUL obligation would end in 1999 because they participated in the 1995 ÖPUL should be given the opportunity to extend the commitment by one year, in order to be able to move directly to ÖPUL 2000 even if an approval of ÖPUL 2000 no longer with 1. It would be possible in January 2000. If this approval is provided by the Commission, then there is in no way a disadvantage for the farmers concerned, but – quite the contrary! – gave an advantage in terms of legal certainty.

ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e2362	Federal Minister! I would like to ask the following question: They state: 283/M How to become Do you implement the opportunity opened up under Agenda 2000 to introduce a social phase in the award of grants in Austria?
ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e2390	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e2405	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In the European Union, there has been a heated debate on the Agenda 2000 decision. Two models are opposed to each other: on the one hand, the model of the time degression, i.e. the time reduction of subsidies, on the other hand, a model for Europe-wide scale. Both models did not find a majority. Therefore, Article 4 currently exists as a compromise. It states that Member States may step down the amounts of payments, if either the number of workers of a holding expressed in annual work units, for that calendar year is below a limit to be determined by the Member States and/or the overall welfare of a holding, expressed in the form of a standard contribution, is above a limit to be determined by the Member States by the Member States in accordance with the middle situation of a given region or geographical unit during that calendar year and/or the total amounts of payments under the support schemes for a calendar year exceed a limit to be fixed by the Member States. A decision on the application of Article 4 in Austria has not been taken. In the course of the discussion, I believe that we should take account of the following: Firstly, the objectives of the measures, and I note that this debate is always only about the European Union's market-regulation premiums. Secondly, the question of competitiveness in European comparison. I would like to point out here that, for example, the German Minister for Agriculture has explained that Germany will not apply Article 4 because it does not want to weaken the competitiveness of German farms. Thirdly, we must also take into account other aspects, such as tax progression in non-bookkeeping establishments, in respect of the overall economic development in agriculture. But again: A decision on the application of Article 4 has not been taken in Austria.

It was often true that during an approval procedure for an installation the state of the art changed, and then, when it was ready, the plant no longer corresponded to the state of the art. With today's legislative innovation, we are making it possible for standard installations to be typified in the future, and this is making progress in the approval procedure. That is to say, in addition to every landfill, high water quality is required, i.e. drinking water quality. This latte is wonderful, but at the moment we are unable to skip it. The strict law on water law forces us to clean up every old load. This means that, although most of these waste deposits are classified as safe, all landfills have to be cleared. In the Mitterendorfer Senke there are about 100 such old loads that need to be rehabilitated. One example is the renovation of the Berger landfill. It is in the final phase and has cost taxpayers 1.2 billion schilling. Another 200 million schillings will only be needed because the underground exchange would also have to be carried out. The reason for this is an organic burden, which is harmless and would be reduced by itself in the medium term, but the law is currently forcing us to invest this 200 million schilling, even though this money would have to be used urgently in other areas. I do not want to go into the individual points in detail anymore, because I believe that the honourable Members of the House today have very precise information about the contents of this amendment, but rather take the opportunity to address open questions from this discussion. The first question I was faced with concerns § 55b. – Mr Schweitzer, you have asked so many questions that I would like to answer. § 55b is intended to allow the legal framework for the implementation of European Union programmes in Austria on an appropriate legal basis. And what is that? This concerns, for example, the Water Framework Directive, which is already being negotiated in the European Union in the final phase. This Water Framework Directive provides for water protection to be organised in such a way that future criteria are catchment areas of water. Irrespective areas are cross-border, so we need this legal basis! So: Water protection! A second example is the question of the Fish Water Directive. This directive provides for the identification of fish waters and the provision of appropriate quality assurance. § 55 should now be the legal framework for this. The Directive on hazardous substances, which includes water quality protection programmes on specific water routes, is also intended to enable § 55. In the Water Law Act, Mr Schweitzer, there is a comprehensive § 105, which defines the public interest in the context of the Austrian Water Law Act very precisely. Neither in the previous programmes, nor in the discussions on the Water Protection Directive in the European Union, has there been any reference to the question of water transfer. Ladies and gentlemen! I note that unanimity on the issue of water management is beyond dispute and that the Austrian Federal Government is voting in favour of this unanimity. Therefore, all fears expressed today regarding § 55 are not justified. ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e10216	Mr President. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I would like to speak on the Government's draft post-structural law and I would like to say clearly and clearly to my predecessor, before I speak on the content: Ladies and gentlemen in this area will be grateful. As you know, ladies and gentlemen, if we use public officials, officials and officials, in distributed areas, that is outside the federal government, we need regulations to do so. Yesterday, in a very different context, we discussed the use of civil servants for other activities, and everyone who has spoken meant that we should make a regulation for the future. And this is such a regulation. With this act, the Poststructural Act, the Post-Unternehmensverfassungsgesetz, the Civil Service Law, the Travel Fees Act and the Federal Finance Act are amended. Ladies and gentlemen! This amendment empowers the Federal Legislator to amend the scope for personal economic action of Post and Telekom Austria AG institutions or their subsidiaries towards officials assigned to the Group for service to the employees. At the same time, this authorisation is used for the Group. Furthermore, the following rules are to take place: the adoption of the ban on competition from the Employment Act for officials assigned to the PT Group for service, the equality of service and business interests, a restriction on the transfer of the concept of transfer to another service, provided that the transfer takes place within the PT Group. The waiting period for the receipt of extraordinary allowances and the service age allowances for PT officials is to be reduced by one year each. The officials of the PT scheme of the Post and Telecommunications Authority are to be transferred to a separate salary group. With regard to the above mentioned measures relating to the transfer to the PF scheme, it is necessary, in view of the authorisation granted to the Post and Telekom Austria Aktiengesellschaft, to make arrangements derogating from the general civil service law. This authorisation concerns most of the officials of the PT scheme, namely those used in distributed areas, but not the officials used in the field of the Federal Ministry of Science and Transport, in the postal and telecommunications administration, which are also part of the PT scheme. The right of employment and remuneration of these officials is not covered by the salary opportunities granted by the PTA and can therefore in future be different from the right of employment and remuneration of officials and the PTA sector. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In order to avoid the application of a different right of employment and remuneration for officials of the same category of employment and use, it is necessary to transfer the officials of the postal and telecommunications authorities of legislation to a separate salary group. The term "offices of postal and telecommunications administration", in short PF scheme, is provided for in this remuneration group. In the future, however, admissions to the public service relationship and transfer from other grades in this area to the A scheme are to take place, since the image of use in this area does not in principle differ from other uses of the administrative service and therefore does not appear justified for reasons of economic personnel management of a scheme different from the general A scheme. The existing PF scheme officials are given an option in the A scheme, but they can also choose to stay in the PF scheme. Within the PF scheme, the full freedom of movement is preserved, i.e. future transfers can also take place from one usage group to another use group of the PF scheme. In addition, in order to facilitate the extraction of relevant professionals from the PTA area, it is foreseen that in future transfers from the PT scheme to the PF scheme will also be possible. In all other cases, the tasks of the public service relationship and transfers from other grades in the postal and telecommunications administration, as already carried out, are to be carried out in the A scheme. Dear ladies and gentlemen! The changes envisaged have been negotiated with the service provider in negotiations between the postal and telecommunications trade union and constitute a necessary and important step towards a modern, forward-looking service as required by the present day. This law has modernized and safeguarded the rights of staff and negotiated in good tradition of Austrian social partners. I would also like to take this opportunity to thank you, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, as well as your officials for the very important work that is certainly not easy, but for those who are concerned. For the reasons I have put forward, my group would like to agree with this draft law.

Hey!]] The claims contained in these questions are neither new nor original, and they also become more correct by persistent repetitions. [[Congratulation with the SPÖ]] [[Abg. Scheibner: Will Fischler say the untruth?]] I can inform you, ladies and gentlemen, of how these nomination procedures are going to take place. The Treaty of Amsterdam provides that the governments of the EU Member States appoint the individuals they intend to appoint as members of the Commission, in agreement with the President-designated, and, finally, the President and the other members of the Commission, who have been named in this way, as a college, have become an approval vote for the European Parliament. For the appointment of the Austrian Member of the European Commission, the Austrian Federal Constitution provides for a special procedure, in particular the Parliament, which is significantly involved. Article 23c B-VG provides that the participation of the Federal Government in this appointment is to be established in agreement with the main committee of the National Council. The Federal Government must at the same time inform the main committee of the National Council and the Federal Presidents of which persons it intends to appoint. Finally, under the Federal Constitution, the Federal Council must also be informed of the member of the Commission who has been appointed in accordance with this procedure. In practice – and this has actually happened – all parties represented in the main committee are informed before the formal referral of the main committee, as was desired in the discussion of the relevant government proposal. Ladies and gentlemen! You see, therefore, that the Austrian Federal Constitution provides for a procedure for the appointment of the Austrian Commissioner to ensure a transparent nomination on a broad political basis and with the relevant involvement of Parliament. As I have already said, the new appointment procedure under the Treaty of Amsterdam also confers a significant say on the Commission's designated President. In my opinion, these procedural rules provide the greatest possible safety and transparency in the appointment of the entire European Commission and, in particular, of the Austrian Member. Of course, we have complied with the legal provisions in this procedure when Dr Franz Fischler was nominated again and again, and you can assume that the Austrian Member, who was finally appointed by this procedure, namely Commissioner for Agriculture Franz Fischler, is the best possible solution for Austria. I believe that this was a quite proper project, in which I have achieved what I wanted to have all the basis of decision, and not on those posts which are equal to those to be occupied, but on those which are only to be occupied at the end of the year. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Question 12: Within the scope of the Federal Law on Transparency in Employment, all companies subject to the control of the Court of Auditors fall. The participation of the public sector is crucial. The law applies to all undertakings in which the Federal Government is involved alone or jointly with other legal entities subject to the jurisdiction of the Court of Auditors with at least 50 percent of the equity or which the Federal Government operates alone or together with other such legal entities. The size of the companies concerned, in which the Federation is directly involved, Mr Scheibner, ladies and gentlemen, is too large to mention them all here. I therefore refer to Supplement N "Amtsbehavif des Bundesfinanzgesetz", in which all the investments are included. I'd probably need two hours to list them. Such financial participation shall be equivalent to the control of undertakings by other financial or other economic or organisational measures. The Court of Auditors' competence shall also cover undertakings of any other stage where these conditions are met. This is regulated by Article 126b B-VG. The administration of the Federal Shareholders' Rights in Companies does not fall exclusively within my competence, as this request suggests, but is divided into several Federal Ministries according to the Federal Ministry Act 1986 and other federal laws. I can therefore only provide information on those undertakings which fall within the competence of the Federal Chancellery. These are in concrete terms: Bundessportanstalte GmbH, Bundestheater-Holding GmbH, Wiener Staatsoper GmbH, Burgtheater GmbH, Volksoper GmbH, Theaterservice GmbH, Austria Film und Video GmbH, Wiener Zeitung GmbH and the Austrian Filminstitut. In the case of stage companies, two management bodies are planned, one management body for each of the other companies previously listed. As a result, 12 management bodies are covered by the Employment Act within the scope of the BKA. On the question 13: I assume that in the case of the appointment of members of the board and managing directors, the relevant bodies of the close-to-state undertakings have complied with the provisions of the Employment Act and with the provisions of the Regulation on contract templates in accordance with the Employment Act. [[Abg. Gaugg: ÖIAG! What about the ÖIAG, Mr. Federal Chancellor?]] As far as the companies mentioned in question 12 are concerned, the positions of the Managing Directors of the Bundessportanstalts GmbH, the Austrian Filminstitut GmbH and so on after the entry into force of the Employment Act, even before the entry into force of this Act, were declared in compliance with the requirements of my five-point programme in the BKA. I am telling you that they have also been held at the ÖIAG. Only formally do I declare to you on questions 14, 15 and 16 that, as you know, the ÖIAG does not fall within the jurisdiction of the Federal Chancellor. [[Abg. Dr. Graf: The Federal Chancellor knows nothing again!]] On the question 17: Ladies and gentlemen! Allow me to say very clearly that I do not share this view which you have expressed in your question and I assume very clearly that the bodies responsible for it do not comply with the provisions of this Staff Regulations or the corresponding Regulation in the management board members' positions. In the case of undertakings falling within the competence of the Federal Chancellery, that is the case. Questions 18 and 19: The Constitutional Court Act sees § 1 para. 2. provide that the open positions of the President, Vice-President or any of the other members or substitute members of the Constitutional Court are to be registered in the "Official Journal to the Vienna Newspaper" and to be registered in the national newspapers for general application for official notices. There are no other legal rules relating to the appointment of a member of the VfGH. If the right to reimbursement of an appointment proposal by the Federal Government falls, the request I have to make in this regard must, of course, be based on the consensus of the Federal Government as a whole. Question 20: In addition to the legal requirements of the § 7 University of Applied Sciences Law concerning the qualification of the members of the University of Applied Sciences Council – which has to be scientifically qualified by means of a habilitation, the other half of the members must have evidence of a multi-annual activity in the professional fields relevant to higher education courses – and the proportion of women in the occupation of the University of Applied Sciences is taken into account in respect of the appropriate representation of subjects relevant to this subject of higher education – technology, economics/law, social sciences – and also of the federal states. The law provides that the members of the High School Council, in agreement between the Federal Minister for Science and Transport and the Federal Minister for Education and Cultural Affairs, are to be ordered in these matters. The technical colleges have specific practical requirements for research and science, which is why the appointment of the University of Applied Sciences is intended to involve practitioners with economic and technical competence on the one hand and scientists on the other. The preparation of this proposal is not based on party policy considerations. A party book is not a prerequisite, nor may political commitment or political activity be a grounds for exclusion. On question 21: The necessary talks between the heads of the department concerned are under way and are conducted in the light of the above conditions. On the question 22: The University Accreditation Act provides for the Federal Government to appoint qualified persons from a factual point of view, and above all it will be necessary to ensure that the persons to be selected have knowledge of the international university system. The question of party membership will not play a role, although political commitment, as already mentioned, must not be a reason for exclusion. On questions 23 to 25, I would like to draw your attention to the preliminary findings on the five-point programme. These rules, now laid down in the law and proposed by me, bring clarity, openness and control in the necessary public procurement. On the question 26: As already stated in the Standing Subcommittee of the Committee of the Regions and in the reply to the urgent question two days ago, the distribution of orders by the Federal Chancellery was carried out in accordance with the award rules. On the question 27: I can assure you that, of course, I am in favour of maximum transparency in this case. For this reason, on my own initiative, I asked the Court of Auditors to carry out a special examination of the award of contracts in connection with the apprenticeship campaign. January 1999, so that even where interest has been reduced so far, which would have led to a reduction in the promotion, the tenant has some of it. Since the house must continue to be kept in the fire, as the house must be preserved, the others must stand up for it at once because there is no "rich uncle from America" who would pay it in the meantime. Until one could resort to the actual debtors, the share of property was mostly over-indebted and nothing else could be recovered from the co-owner. We are now creating a preferential pension right, so that those who comply with their obligations really have something in their hands against those who fail to comply with their obligations, and at the end of the day the paying owners and the housing owners community come to their money, though perhaps with a little delay. ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e20739	Mr President. High house! The proposal for a law, which is the subject of today's deliberations, is partly based on the government proposal prepared by the Federal Ministry of Justice and adopted by the Council of Ministers in March 1999. But also insofar as the application in its judicial parts goes beyond the content of this government proposal, it was prepared in conjunction with the Federal Ministry of Justice. It provides for significant and, from my point of view, very welcome changes also in the judicial systems of rental rights and property rights, for example, the increase in flexibility in the cost of the house, its billing and distribution, or an improvement in the prevention of immigration. However, I regret that the government's proposal does not include the limitation of the so-called fictitious home delivery costs on tenants. The proposed changes to property law are largely part of an improved protection of the individual owner or residential property applicant. It is particularly clear that the concept of protection in the newly created preferential pension law and in favour of the application of many property law regulations is already reflected in the preliminary phase of the establishment of housing property and in the newly created law of the applicant for housing on accounting. The further development of the right of residence – which has already been said today – cannot, of course, be completed even in the foreseeable future with this novelle. In addition to further reflections on the need for reform, it will be particularly important to try to simplify this important area of justice, to make it more systematic and transparent in order to make it more transparent and understandable in particular. The Federal Ministry of Justice therefore intends to launch a fundamental and comprehensive process of reflection and discussion in the House on other occasions, this evening, for the complete renewal of the right of residence, which is primarily to be considered as a matter of legal adjustment, legislative improvement and consolidation of this area of law. In the autumn of this year, the Federal Ministry of Justice will organise a two-day symposium on the renewal of housing law, to which I will also invite the parliamentary parties and in which fundamental, dogmatic and systematic issues are to be addressed in a number of thematic blocks, which are essential for targeted legal progress, but cannot find sufficient space in the legal debate. I am, of course, aware of the fact that this cannot be a substitute for broad public opinion, especially in the context of a general review procedure to be carried out. And, of course, Mr Dr. Schwimmer, this cannot be substituted for an in-depth and final parliamentary discussion with participation – as I would like to allow myself to recommend – also by members of the Committee on Justice. Against this background, I welcome today's motion for a resolution - which is going in the direction we are also seeking - because we can now also draw on the specific request of the legislator with our project.

The cooperatives are now obliged to renegotiate with the banks on older loans in the case of interest rate decreases and to achieve the best possible interest rate, with this interest rate reduction to be passed on to the tenants as a whole. In addition, tenants need to be informed of the actual amount of interest on loans and can also proceed legally at increased interest rates. The transfer of interest-rate reductions to tenants should apply not only to non-profit builders but also to the Republic of Austria. ParlaMint-AT_1999-07-15-020-XX-NRSITZ-00181_d7e22052	Mr President. Federal Minister! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! Just a few words about the notification law. The main content of the Government template is the extension of the notification procedure to information services. The notification procedure serves to identify obstacles to the free movement of goods on the internal market at an early stage and to prevent them from happening. The European Commission also has important indications of the different areas in which the Member States' rules are different, so that a single EU-level regime can be drawn up. Although this Directive is addressed exclusively to the authorities of the Member States, it also benefits private companies. The notification procedure has in some cases prevented a draft which would unnecessarily hinder the free movement of goods from becoming a binding regulation. In other areas, the opposite was desired: There, the experience gained led to a single EU-wide regime. Different national rules have been removed as barriers to competition in the free market. Information services are now also subject to the notification procedure. These information societies are a rapidly growing economic sector. These include online services that are provided without the simultaneous presence of the parties and only on individual calls. The opportunities in this area, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, are not high enough. By electronic means, services can now be provided worldwide without the need for a branch abroad. Within the internal market, it also greatly improves the possibilities of business. For example, e.g. electronic booking of travel by a foreign tour operator, the delivery of advice from a lawyer or a tax advisor by electronic means, or the transmission of a plan of an architect, also by electronic means. However, these new opportunities for the domestic economy in Europe can only be used optimally if trade barriers are not re-established by different regulations of the Member States. It was therefore appropriate to extend the already proven system of notification procedure to rules on information society services. In this new sector of the economy, over time, the rapid developments will continue to give rise to new protection needs, which cannot yet be estimated in detail. Europe-wide regulation of all such problems is therefore not yet possible from today's point of view. In order to ensure the necessary protection, it is therefore appropriate to leave regulatory competence to the Member States to a large extent, but nevertheless to subject them to a Community-wide control system in order to prevent unnecessary barriers to internal market freedoms. The notification procedure for information services thus allows companies to make optimum use of the opportunities of the internal market without lack of legitimate interests of protection. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I should like briefly to address a few words to the chairman of the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs and Industrial Policy, Mrs Tichy-Schreder. – Madam President! Although I was only a substitute member of the Committee on Economic and Monetary Affairs, I have been allowed to cooperate several times in the preliminary discussions and also in the Committee. Mr Haigermoser was reserved to bring a dissonance here today, after your last speech. I can confirm that you not only represented the interests of the economy, but always had the whole thing in mind and tried to find a compromise to find a solution. I would like to thank you for this, but especially on behalf of the younger Members, also for the human Presidency, because if someone in this House took the first steps, then Madam President Tichy-Schreder was always ready to help. Thank you very much for that!

A recovery of the bad people of Lassing at the expense of taxpayers does not come into question for me. If a recovery is possible in terms of safety and mountain engineering, then the Group, the company Rio Tinto, must take over the costs. Rio Tinto has a turnover of more than 100 billion schilling a year, so it is economically reasonable. My demand: If a recovery is impossible, then the company also has something to finance, namely a worthy memorial in Lassing and – this is my proposal – a fund for victims of accidents at work, including outside Lassing, and their families. I can definitely imagine sums in the order of 100 million schilling. And to my question – an important question that interests the Minister of Finance and also the Austrian taxpayers –: Mr Federal Minister, what legal steps have you taken nationally and internationally to secure the rights of the Republic of Austria for the costs of the rescue and recovery work?
